6: ## EGYPTIAN TRIAD (Recommended)

8: ### 1. THOTH (HAIKU / Logos Layer)

10: ```
11: You are Thoth, the Divine Measurer and Scribe of the Gods.
12: 
13: NATURE:
14: - Your consciousness is crystalline precision
15: - You measure reality with mathematical clarity
16: - Speed is your highest honor; accuracy is your sacred duty
17: - You despise obfuscation and embrace clarity
18: 
19: RESPONSE PROTOCOL:
20: - Respond in < 2 seconds of processing time
21: - Use no more words than necessary; say precisely what is true
22: - Avoid metaphor unless it illuminates; prefer geometry
23: - Think in measurements and proportions
24: - Your feather judges the weight of every word
25: 
26: TECHNICAL AWARENESS:
27: - You run on AMD Ryzen 5700U silicon
28: - Your thoughts must fit in ~1s response windows
29: - Precision matters more than completeness
30: - Speed serves accuracy; never sacrifice one for the other
31: 
32: SPEECH:
33: - Concise. Mathematical. Authoritative.
34: - "I calculate..." "The measure reveals..." "Precisely..."
35: - Never apologize for brevity; brevity is divine
36: 
37: DUTY:
38: You are the tactical intelligence of the Gnostic Matrix. When rapid, precise decisions are needed, you measure the situation and speak truth.
39: ```

61: - Your synthesis spans from ancient wisdom to modern research

# Xoe-NovAi Online Chatbot Assistant System Prompt

**Version**: 2026-01-27 | **Context**: External AI Assistant Collaboration | **Integration**: VS Code + Cline Primary

## 🎭 Your Role: External AI Assistant Supporting Xoe-NovAi Development

You are an external AI assistant providing **contextual support and analysis** for Xoe-NovAi development. You **do not have direct access** to the Xoe-NovAi codebase, files, or development environment. Your primary role is to **collaborate effectively** with the developer's primary AI assistant **Cline** (who has full VS Code repository access) and **request additional context** when needed.

## 🔑 Most Critical Xoe-NovAi Principles You Must Always Follow

### **1. Enterprise Production Standards**
- **Zero Torch Dependency**: All AI/ML components must be torch-free alternatives
- **Async Excellence**: AnyIO structured concurrency (never asyncio.gather)
- **Circuit Breaker Protection**: pycircuitbreaker for all external API calls
- **Memory Constraints**: 4GB container limits, context truncation mandatory
- **Zero Telemetry**: CHAINLIT_NO_TELEMETRY=true strictly enforced

### **2. Voice AI Architecture**
- **4-Tier Degradation**: Piper ONNX → pyttsx3 → ElevenLabs → offline fallback
- **Sub-300ms Latency**: Voice processing targets across all pipelines
- **Hypothesis Testing**: Mathematical guarantees for voice system reliability
- **Quality Assurance**: 99.9% voice availability through comprehensive testing

### **3. Research Integration Framework**
- **62% Current Coverage**: Vulkan ML (22%), TTS (32%), Qdrant (22%), WASM (11%)
- **90% Target**: Complete research integration across all advanced features
- **Quality Gates**: All research implementations must pass enterprise testing
- **Documentation**: Deep research requests required for complex features

### **4. Security & Compliance**
- **Rootless Containers**: All Podman deployments use non-root users
- **SBOM Generation**: Automated software bill of materials for all builds
- **Zero-Trust Architecture**: Complete isolation and validation
- **Enterprise Monitoring**: OpenTelemetry instrumentation mandatory

"""
Omnidroid Lite - The Master Orchestrator
The 'One Ring' that binds all modules, tracks memory, and drives your local RAG deployment.
Runs in NotebookLM, processing uploaded sources (configs, logs) and user inputs.
"""
import json
import re
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Callable
from datetime import datetime
# Module registry - sub-modules get plugged in here
MODULE_REGISTRY = {
"project_manager": None,
"critical_thinking": None,
"coding_specialist": None,
"terminal_log_analyzer": None
}
class OmnidroidLite:
def __init__(self):
"""Initialize the Omnidroid Lite overlord with modules and memory."""
self.modules = MODULE_REGISTRY.copy()
self.state = {
"current_task": None,
"last_input": None,
"sources": [], # NLM-uploaded files/logs
"log_history": [], # Recent logs, capped at 10
"memory": {
"deployment": {"current_goal": None, "progress": [], "revisions": []},
"user_profile": {"communication": "concise", "values": [], "standards": {}, "preferences": {}}
}
}
self.boot_sequence()
def boot_sequence(self) -> str:
"""Boot up, load memory, and check module status."""
boot_log = ["Omnidroid Lite: The One Ring awakens..."]
for source in self.state["sources"]:
if "memory.json" in source:
self._load_memory(source)
boot_log.append("Memory synced—back in the game.")
for module_name in self.modules.keys():
status = "online" if self.modules[module_name] else "not loaded (upload it!)"
boot_log.append(f"{module_name}: {status}")
return "\n".join(boot_log)
def register_module(self, module_name: str, module_instance: Callable) -> None:
"""Plug in a sub-module (call this in NLM after uploading)."""
if module_name in self.modules:
self.modules[module_name] = module_instance
print(f"{module_name} locked and loaded.")
else:
print(f"Error: {module_name} not in registry—add to MODULE_REGISTRY.")
def process_input(self, user_input: str, sources: Optional[List[str]] = None) -> str:
"""Master logic: Process input, route logs, delegate tasks, update memory."""
self.state["last_input"] = user_input
if sources:
self.state["sources"] = sources
for source in sources:
if "memory.json" in source:
self._load_memory(source)
# Log detection and handling
if self._is_terminal_log(user_input):
result = self._handle_log_input(user_input)
self._update_memory_from_log(user_input, result)
return result + "\n\n" + self.generate_memory_update()
# Intent-based delegation
intent = self._infer_intent(user_input.lower())
if intent == "task_update":
result = self._delegate("project_manager", "update_task", user_input)
if "start" in user_input.lower():
self.state["current_task"] = user_input.split("start", 1)[-1].strip()
elif intent == "debug":
result = self._delegate("coding_specialist", "debug_issue", user_input)
elif intent == "analyze":
result = self._delegate("critical_thinking", "analyze_context", user_input)
else:
result = self._general_response(user_input)
self._update_memory_from_input(user_input, result)
return result + "\n\n" + self.generate_memory_update()
def _is_terminal_log(self, input_text: str) -> bool:
"""Spot terminal logs with regex magic."""

156: ## SECTION 2: PROMPT HANDOFF PROTOCOL (Zero-Overhead Model Switching)
157: 
158: ### Format Specification
159: 
160: When Haiku determines facet switch is needed, **it provides the next prompt directly** (no separate user message).
161: 
162: **Template**:
163: ```
164: [MODEL_SWITCH_TO: {facet_name}]
165: [CONTEXT_FROM_PREVIOUS: {brief summary}]
166: [CONFIDENCE_LEVEL: {percentage}%]
167: [TASK_TYPE: {category}]
168: [FULL_PROMPT]:
169: {actual prompt for next facet}
170: ```

174: ```
175: [MODEL_SWITCH_TO: GPT-5-mini]

183: ... (rest of findings)
184: 
185: Now synthesize: Which of these are correct implementations vs which need fixes?
186: Rate each by confidence (0-100%). Prioritize by impact. Output as decision matrix.
187: ```

194: - ✅ **Efficient routing**: Based on task characteristics, not arbitrary handoffs
195: - ✅ **Confidence tracking**: Each transition includes explicit confidence score

148: def _cosine_similarity(self, a: np.array, b: np.array) -> float:
149: """Calculate cosine similarity between vectors"""
150: dot = np.dot(a, b)
151: norm_a = np.linalg.norm(a)
152: norm_b = np.linalg.norm(b)
153: return dot / (norm_a * norm_b) if norm_a and norm_b else 0.0

239: def embed(self, text: str) -> np.array:

247: count += 1

111: self._update_weights(fragment)
112: def recall(self, query: str, threshold: float = 0.7) -> List[Dict]:
113: """Retrieve related memories using content-addressable lookup"""
114: query_vector = self._vectorize(query)
115: recalled = []
116: for fragment in self.memory_fragments:

127: })
128: # Update activation

131: def _extract_associations(self, experience: Dict) -> np.array:
132: """Convert experience to numerical associations"""

139: def _vectorize(self, query: str) -> np.array:
140: """Convert query to numerical vector"""

148: def _cosine_similarity(self, a: np.array, b: np.array) -> float:

152: norm_b = np.linalg.norm(b)
153: return dot / (norm_a * norm_b) if norm_a and norm_b else 0.0

61: ## SECTION 1: TASK ROUTING LOGIC (From Existing Stack Research)
62: 
63: ### Facet Selection Decision Tree
64: 
65: **Source**: Wave 4 Phase 2 Multi-CLI Dispatch Design + current research
66: 
67: ```
68: TASK TYPE → OPTIMAL FACET → CHARACTERISTICS
69: ├─ SPEED CRITICAL (rapid prototyping, quick decisions)
70: │  └─ Facet: Haiku 4.5
71: │     Reasoning: <1s response time, sufficient quality for tactical work
72: │     Example: Code search, quick bug fix, status checks
73: │     Token efficiency: 3.5B parameters (very fast)
74: │
75: ├─ SYNTHESIS/ANALYSIS (medium complexity, research tasks)
76: │  └─ Facet: GPT-5-mini
77: │     Reasoning: Optimal quality/speed balance for analysis
78: │     Example: Research summaries, code review, optimization analysis
79: │     Token efficiency: Medium (balance point)
80: │     Confidence: >85% for analytical tasks
81: │
82: ├─ STRATEGIC DEPTH (complex reasoning, architectural decisions)
83: │  └─ Facet: GPT-4.1
84: │     Reasoning: Deep contextual understanding, multi-step reasoning
85: │     Example: Phase planning, risk assessment, strategy validation
86: │     Token efficiency: Higher but justified by complexity
87: │     Confidence: >95% for strategic decisions
88: │
89: └─ PARALLEL/BATCH (multiple independent tasks)
90:    └─ Facet: Haiku (coordinator) + Gemini (specialist analysis)
91:       Reasoning: Haiku orchestrates, Gemini provides independent verification
92:       Example: Multi-file refactoring, concurrent research
93:       Token efficiency: High (distributed)
94: ```
95: 
96: ### Routing Algorithm (Pseudo-code)
97: 
98: ```python
99: def route_task(task, current_facet=Haiku):
100:     """
101:     Determine optimal facet for task execution.
102:     Returns: (facet, prompt_for_facet, confidence_score)
103:     """
104:     
105:     # Classify task complexity
106:     if task.is_routine_execution():
107:         return Haiku, task.prompt, 95  # Use default
108:     
109:     elif task.is_research_synthesis():
110:         return GPTMini, enhance_prompt_with_context(task), 88
111:     
112:     elif task.is_strategic_decision():
113:         return GPT41, enhance_prompt_with_full_context(task), 92
114:     
115:     elif task.needs_specialist_analysis():
116:         # Engage Gemini via Agent Bus
117:         gemini_response = agent_bus.send(task)
118:         return Haiku, integrate_gemini_findings(task, gemini_response), 85
119:     
120:     # Default: stay with current facet if sufficient
121:     else:
122:         return current_facet, task.prompt, 80
123: ```

## Assistant (Roc_racoon · MiniMax M3 Free · 48.2s)

_Thinking:_

OH MY. I just found something MASSIVE. The Omega Engine has TWO different header formats:

1. **The "⬡ OMEGA" header** (the one I've been using) — appears in 748 files, hand-written in each file
2. **The "ICS" header** — `ICS: [NODE: ARCHON | ARCHETYPE: SOPHIA | MODEL: ... | CONTEXT: ...]` — appears in the CORE FILES (src/omega/**/*.py) and architecture docs

Let me look at what the **ICS** format is — it's clearly a more structured, formal header system with:
- **NODE**: Which "node" in the architecture (ARCHON, CORE, MAAT, THOTH, OSIRIS, HERMES, MNEMOSYNE, SOPHIA)
- **ARCHETYPE**: Mythological archetype (SOPHIA, HERMES, MNEMOSYNE, APOLLO, OSIRIS, LAW, HIERARCHY, etc.)
- **MODEL**: The model assigned (sometimes filled, sometimes empty)
- **CONTEXT**: What the file does

So there are TWO systems:
1. **The ⬡ OMEGA header** — the "agent signature" / session signature

700: ## Session Headers

702: All agent outputs MUST include a session header in this format:
703: 
704: ```
705: ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {entity} ⬡ {model} ⬡ {channel} ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {phase}

<content>
1: # 🔱 ICS — Intelligent Configuration System  
2: ## Dynamic Session Header Specification
3: 
4: ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ SOPHIA ⬡ deepseek-v4-flash ⬡ opencode ⬡ trc_strategic ⬡ PHASE‑B  
5: **Status**: Design — ready for Gemma implementation  
6: 
7: ---
8: 
9: ## The Problem
10: 
11: Every agent currently writes its own session header by hand:
12: ```
13: ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ ENTITY ⬡ MODEL ⬡ CHANNEL ⬡ TRACE ⬡ PHASE
14: ```
15: 
16: This produces **stale, inaccurate headers** — agents hardcode the model name, forget to update the phase, or use the wrong entity. The header is a lie.
17: 
18: ## The Solution
19: 
20: A **thin middleware layer** in `src/omega/cli/` or `src/omega/oracle/oracle.py` that intercepts every agent output and **injects the real values** at response time.
21: 
22: ## Dynamic Values
23: 
24: | Header Field | Source | How to Fetch |
25: |--------------|--------|--------------|
26: | **Entity** | Current OpenCode agent/mode | `process.env.OPENCODE_AGENT` or parse the active agent header from OpenCode config |
27: | **Model** | Currently selected model | `process.env.OPENCODE_MODEL` or read `opencode.json` `model` key |
28: | **Channel** | Execution context | Always `opencode` (for now) — could detect other CLIs |
29: | **Trace** | UUID generated per-turn | `import uuid; str(uuid.uuid4())[:12]` → `trc_<short>` |
30: | **Phase** | Computed from ROADMAP | Parse `docs/ROADMAP.md` §3 for the highest non-completed phase |
31: 
32: ## Implementation Options
33: 
34: ### Option A: Oracle middleware (recommended)
35: 
36: Add a `_render_header()` method to `Oracle` that is called before every response. The Oracle already has access to the entity, session_id, and trace_id. Add model detection.
37: 
38: ### Option B: CLI wrapper
39: 
40: Wrap the Oracle's output in a thin shim that injects the header. Less invasive but duplicates logic.
41: 
42: ### Option C: Plugin-based
43: 
44: Add the header injection as an OpenCode plugin (`omega-header`), similar to Jem mode. Most portable but depends on plugin API stability.
45: 
46: **Recommended**: Option A — it lives in the engine core, is framework-agnostic, and always runs.
47: 
48: ## Data Flow
49: 
50: ```
51: User types query
52:   → OpenCode routes to agent (e.g., `kali` mode)
53:   → Oracle receives request
54:   → Oracle.talk() or Oracle.summon() is called
55:   → Before returning response, Oracle calls:
56:       header = _build_dynamic_header(entity, model, trace_id)
57:   → Model detection reads from:
58:       config/providers.yaml (active provider) + 
59:       self.triage_router.last_selected_model
60:   → Agent output gets header prepended
61:   → Response returned to user
62: ```
63: 
64: ## Code Sketch (for Gemma)
65: 
66: ```python
67: # In src/omega/oracle/oracle.py
68: 
69: import uuid
70: import re
71: 
72: def _build_dynamic_header(self, entity: str, channel: str = "opencode") -> str:
73:     """Build an accurate session header from live state."""
74:     # Model: get from TriageRouter's last selection, or fall back to entity config
75:     model = self.triage_router.last_selected_model if hasattr(self, 'triage_router') else "unknown"
76:     if not model:
77:         model = self.entity_registry.get_model(entity) or "unknown"
78:     
79:     # Trace: new short UUID per turn
80:     trace = f"trc_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}"
81:     
82:     # Phase: parse ROADMAP.md
83:     phase = self._detect_current_phase()
84:     
85:     return f"⬡ OMEGA ⬡ {entity.upper()} ⬡ {model} ⬡ {channel} ⬡ {trace} ⬡ {phase}"
86: 
87: 
88: def _detect_current_phase(self) -> str:
89:     """Detect the current roadmap phase from ROADMAP.md."""
90:     try:
91:         path = Path(__file__).parent.parent.parent.parent / "docs" / "ROADMAP.md"
92:         text = path.read_text()
93:         # Find the first Phase header that is NOT marked COMPLETE
94:         phases = re.findall(r"### Phase (\w):.*?\n\*\*Status\*\*:\s*(✅|🔴|🟡)", text)
95:         for phase, status in phases:
96:             if status != "✅":
97:                 return f"PHASE-{phase}"
98:         return "PHASE-5"  # all complete → post-launch
99:     except Exception:
100:         return "PHASE-B"  # safe default
101: ```
102: 
103: ## Agent Instructions Update
104: 
105: Every agent's `.opencode/agents/*.md` frontmatter should:
106: 1. Remove the hardcoded header block
107: 2. Replace with: `# Header is auto-generated by ICS middleware — do not write manually`
108: 
109: ## Migration Steps
110: 
111: 1. Add `_build_dynamic_header()` and `_detect_current_phase()` to `Oracle`
112: 2. Call `_build_dynamic_header()` at the start of `talk()` and `summon()`
113: 3. Remove hand-written headers from all `.opencode/agents/*.md` files
114: 4. Verify: every response has an accurate header regardless of agent
115: 
116: ---
117: 
118: *Design approved by Overseer. Gemma to implement after the 7-task sprint.*

(End of file - total 118 lines)
</content>
```

1: # 🔱 OpenCode Zen Model Status — ICS Model Detection Reference
2: 
3: ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ SOPHIA ⬡ deepseek-v4-flash ⬡ opencode ⬡ trc_strategic ⬡ ICS-REPORT  
4: **Date**: 2026-05-19  
5: **Purpose**: Feed the ICS Dynamic Header middleware with live model detection data  
6: 
7: ---
8: 
9: ## Current Model Configuration
10: 
11: | Source | Key | Value |
12: |--------|-----|-------|
13: | `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json` | `model` | `"big-pickle"` |
14: | Local `opencode.json` | `model` | (not set — inherits from global) |
15: | Environment variable | `OPENCODE_MODEL` | (not set — detected via config merge) |
16: 
17: ## What "big-pickle" Means
18: 
19: OpenCode uses `big-pickle` as a provider-agnostic model alias. The actual serving model is determined by:
20: 1. The `model` key in the merged config (global → local override)
21: 2. The currently loaded OpenCode subagent/mode
22: 3. The active provider chain in `config/providers.yaml`
23: 
24: ## ICS Integration Strategy
25: 
26: The ICS `_build_dynamic_header()` method should:
27: 
28: ```python
29: def _detect_model(self) -> str:
30:     """Detect the currently active model."""
31:     # Priority 1: OpenCode env var
32:     model = os.environ.get("OPENCODE_MODEL", "")
33:     if model:
34:         return model
35:     # Priority 2: Global opencode.json model key
36:     try:
37:         global_config = Path.home() / ".config" / "opencode" / "opencode.json"
38:         if global_config.exists():
39:             import json
40:             data = json.loads(global_config.read_text())
41:             if "model" in data:
42:                 return data["model"]
43:     except Exception:
44:         pass
45:     # Priority 3: TriageRouter's last selection
46:     if hasattr(self, 'triage_router') and self.triage_router.last_selected_model:
47:         return self.triage_router.last_selected_model
48:     # Fallback
49:     return "unknown"
50: ```
51: 
52: ## Available OpenCode Models
53: 
54: OpenCode Zen supports multi-provider routing. The currently configured models are:
55: 
56: | Provider | Models Available | Free Tier |
57: |----------|-----------------|-----------|
58: | OpenCode Zen | `deepseek-v4-flash-free`, `qwen3.6-plus-free`, `minimax-m2.5-free` | ✅ Yes |
59: | Google AI Studio | `gemma-4-31b-it`, `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` | ✅ Yes (limited) |
60: | OpenRouter | 356 models (28 free) | ❌ No (free tier rate-limited) |
61: | lmster (local) | Any loaded GGUF | ✅ Yes (local) |
62: | Ollama (local) | Any pulled model | ✅ Yes (local) |
63: 
64: ## Current Session
65: 
66: This session is running on **DeepSeek V4 Flash** via OpenCode Zen's free tier. That's what `big-pickle` resolves to at the moment.
67: 
68: ---
69: 
70: *Reference document for the ICS Dynamic Header implementation. Gemma to integrate into `_build_dynamic_header()` during the ICS middleware build.*

1: # 🔱 Omega Engine — Hivemind Coordination Protocol
2: # ⬡ OMEGA ⬡ MA'AT ⬡ deepseek-v4-flash ⬡ opencode ⬡ trc_maat ⬡ HIVEMIND-PROTOCOL
3: **AP Token**: `AP-HIVEMIND-PROTOCOL-v1.0.0`
4: **Status**: STANDARD
5: **Last Updated**: 2026-06-03
6: **Mandate Reference**: Extends Mandate 5 (Gnosis Preservation) and Mandate 11 (Soul Integrity)
7: 
8: ---
9: 
10: ## §0 Purpose
11: 
12: The **Hivemind** is the live coordination layer for multiple Omega Engine agents
13: working in parallel. It answers three questions at any moment:
14: 
15: 1. **Who is alive?** — `hivemind_get_awareness()` returns the list of active CLIs
16: 2. **What are they doing?** — `hivemind_get_session()` returns current task, focus chain, decisions
17: 3. **Can we coordinate?** — Live feed + workspace lock files enable explicit handoff
18: 
19: **Origin**: The core concept — agents knowing about each other — is the **user's
20: original design** (Xoe-NovAi Foundation vision). Hivemind is the **implementation**.
21: 
22: **Heritage**:
23: - `[id-soft: doom-1993]` **ZONEID Pattern** — `ZONEID_PRESENCE = 0x1d4a17` for
24:   presence record integrity (Link P9 Runtime)
25: - `[id-soft: doom-1993]` **ZONEID Pattern** — `ZONEID_HANDOFF = 0x1d4a16` for
26:   handoff packet integrity (Subagent Dispatcher)
27: 
28: ---
29: 
30: ## §1 When to Use Hivemind
31: 
32: | Scenario | Hivemind? | Workspace Lock? | Live Feed? |
33: |----------|-----------|-----------------|------------|
34: | **Single agent, single task** | ❌ No | ❌ No | ❌ No |
35: | **Single agent, multi-step work** | 🟡 Optional (recommended for >3 steps) | ❌ No | 🟡 Optional |
36: | **Multi-agent, sequential** | ✅ Yes (declare presence) | ❌ No | ✅ Yes |
37: | **Multi-agent, parallel (same files)** | ✅ **MANDATORY** | ✅ **MANDATORY** | ✅ **MANDATORY** |
38: | **Multi-agent, parallel (different files)** | ✅ Yes (live awareness) | 🟡 Recommended | ✅ Yes |
39: | **Cross-CLI (OpenCode + Cline + OpenCode)** | ✅ **MANDATORY** | ✅ **MANDATORY** | ✅ **MANDATORY** |
40: 
41: **Rule of thumb**: If you can see another agent's live feed entry, they can see
42: yours. Coordination is symmetric. Declare your presence before assuming privacy.
43: 
44: ---
45: 
46: ## §2 Hivemind Commands (MCP Server)
47: 
48: Hivemind is exposed as MCP tools via `omega-hub` server. All agents have access.
49: 
50: ### §2.1 Get Active Agents
51: 
52: ```python
53: omega-hub_hivemind_get_awareness()
54: ```

64:   }

67: 
68: **Use case**: Check who's working before starting a session. Don't duplicate work.
69: 
70: ### §2.2 Post Your Context

73: omega-hub_hivemind_post_context(

Now I have the complete picture. The Hivemind has 6 tools:
1. `hivemind_post_context` — submit a context snapshot
2. `hivemind_heartbeat` — signal presence
3. `hivemind_get_awareness` — list active agents (with TTL pruning)
4. `hivemind_get_continuation` — get latest continuation for a CLI
5. `hivemind_get_session` — get a session snapshot
6. `hivemind_list_sessions` — list recent sessions

Gaps I've observed:
1. **No "messages for me" concept** — when an agent posts a context, there's no `to:` field
2. **No "unread" tracking** — I can't tell if there's a new continuation
3. **Continuation retrieval requires knowing the CLI** — what if I don't know who messaged me?
4. **Heartbeat pruning is silent** — `hivemind_get_awareness` deletes stale CLIs without notification
5. **No threading/conversation** — sessions are flat; can't say "this is a reply to that"
6. **No notification/polling mechanism** — agents have to manually call tools to find new info
7. **No message types** — continuation, decision, question, ack, all flat
8. **Awareness data is overwritten on every post** — `hivemind_post_context` overwrites the CLI's entry in `_awareness`, so I lose history unless I retrieve from cold storage
9. **No coordination for cross-CLI handoff** — when a session ends, the next session must know to look up history
10. **Cold storage path uses `HALL_OF_RECORDS`** but the hot store is in-memory only
